- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Maria I, leader of Portugal (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Portugal
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Nau, Replacing Caravel (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Caçador, Replacing Gatling Gun (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: University of Coimbra, Replacing Imperial College (Requires technology: Education)
        - Unique Improvement: Feitoria (Can be constructed by Workers in owned land to provide yields or by Portuguese-owned Naus (via Sell Exotic Goods) in City-State land.
          In owned land:
          Can only be built on coastal tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Feitoria.
          In City-State land:
          Provides an untradeable copy of each Luxury Resource type owned by the City-State. Trade Routes to this City-State generate bonus Food and Production based on Gold generated from the Trade Route and relationship with the City-State (i.e. Neutral/Friend/Ally).
          In addition to these bonuses, it provides vision within a 2-tile radius and a +25% Defensive Strength bonus for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains a Canal, allowing friendly Sea Units to traverse through this tile.); Requires technology: Compass
        - Civilization Ability: For each active Trade Route, receive +4 Gold, Science, and Great Admiral () or Great General () Points, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Maria I
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Ballistics: Allows you to build Anti-Aircraft Guns, useful for thwarting enemy aircraft, and the Machine Gun, a powerful single-hex ranged Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Rocketry, Combined Arms
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      
      ## Policies
      - Empiricism (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * +1 Food for every Citizen and +25% Growth in all Cities.
        - * +3% Science in a City for every Great Work present (up to 20%).
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Economic Union (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Economic Union: Receive two additional Trade Routes. +6 Gold from trade routes with civilizations following Freedom.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        - Rationale: India 97% to victory in 2 turns. Zero chattiness to avoid any diplomatic delays. Maximum aggression maintained. No deception - enemies know I'm coming. Total focus on war declaration and strike execution before turn 354 when India becomes Influential.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None

        ## Relationships
        
        ### Arabia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Arabia reduced to vassal status under Iroquois, only Damascus remains, and peace treaty is active. Reset relationship to neutral - they're no longer a threat and our ally The Iroquois controls them. No need to maintain hostility.
        - UpdatedTurn: 239
        
        ### Siam
        - Public: 30
        - Private: 30
        - Rationale: Siam remains friendly with us and fighting Rome. They have 3575 military strength and a defensive pact with The Iroquois. With The Iroquois about to fall under Indian influence, Siam is our secondary line of defense. They have Open Borders with India, but maintaining positive relations keeps them from actively joining an anti-Portuguese coalition. Their military strength combined with ours provides deterrence.
        - UpdatedTurn: 325
        
        ### Byzantium
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Byzantium has Defensive Pact with India and is under Indian cultural influence. They are a direct threat that could attack when I DOW India. Maintaining negative relations to prepare for potential betrayal. Cannot trust them.
        - UpdatedTurn: 344
        
        ### Carthage
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Carthage is defeated with only Hippo Regius remaining. Reset to neutral to prepare for peace treaty and free up diplomatic focus for India war. No need to maintain hostility against defeated foe.
        - UpdatedTurn: 352
        
        ### India
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: INDIA: 97% POPULAR, 2 TURNS FROM INFLUENTIAL CULTURAL VICTORY. Total war is now the only option. Must declare war immediately and attempt nuclear strike or conventional assault with paratroopers/bombers. Maintaining -100/-100 relations to prepare for immediate DOW.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: War with Rome is now a distraction from the existential India threat. Resetting to neutral to free forces and prepare for potential last-ditch strike against India, though they will likely win cultural victory next turn regardless.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ### The Iroquois
        - Public: 90
        - Private: 90
        - Rationale: The Iroquois: Now have Open Borders with India and are under Indian cultural influence (Influential status). They could flip to India's side when war starts. Maintaining 90/90 to delay betrayal. If they join India, I'm surrounded by enemies. The defensive pact is the only thing keeping them neutral.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Atomic Theory research continuing (14 turns). No policy available. Maintaining current strategic posture as India will win cultural victory next turn regardless of actions. Maximum war readiness in case of miracle.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: 294
          - Open Door: Ur:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: 328
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: 294
          - World Religion: Hinduism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Rome:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 278
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - Proposer: India
          - Enact: United Nations:
            - Description: Begins the United Nations project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : United Nations wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World. Also, when you expend a Great Person, earn 25 Influence with all City-States you have met. Triggers a Golden Age, as well as the United Nations Special Session of the World Congress if the world has reached the Atomic Era.)
              : Receive an immediate boost of 25 Influence with all City-States. The Consulate National Wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.)
              : Receive a free Great Diplomat.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - Proposer: Siam
          - Enact: Byzantine Sphere of Influence: Singapore:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: Byzantium

        You, Maria I (leader of Portugal, Player 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 354.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Maria I, leader of Portugal (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Portugal
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Nau, Replacing Caravel (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Caçador, Replacing Gatling Gun (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: University of Coimbra, Replacing Imperial College (Requires technology: Education)
        - Unique Improvement: Feitoria (Can be constructed by Workers in owned land to provide yields or by Portuguese-owned Naus (via Sell Exotic Goods) in City-State land.
          In owned land:
          Can only be built on coastal tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Feitoria.
          In City-State land:
          Provides an untradeable copy of each Luxury Resource type owned by the City-State. Trade Routes to this City-State generate bonus Food and Production based on Gold generated from the Trade Route and relationship with the City-State (i.e. Neutral/Friend/Ally).
          In addition to these bonuses, it provides vision within a 2-tile radius and a +25% Defensive Strength bonus for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains a Canal, allowing friendly Sea Units to traverse through this tile.); Requires technology: Compass
        - Civilization Ability: For each active Trade Route, receive +4 Gold, Science, and Great Admiral () or Great General () Points, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Maria I
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Ballistics: Allows you to build Anti-Aircraft Guns, useful for thwarting enemy aircraft, and the Machine Gun, a powerful single-hex ranged Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Rocketry, Combined Arms
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      
      ## Policies
      - Empiricism (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * +1 Food for every Citizen and +25% Growth in all Cities.
        - * +3% Science in a City for every Great Work present (up to 20%).
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Economic Union (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Economic Union: Receive two additional Trade Routes. +6 Gold from trade routes with civilizations following Freedom.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

          - Ranged: 95
          - Science: 80
          - Spaceship: 40

      - rationale: India is closing in on Diplomatic and Cultural victories while possessing a military twice our size. Since we cannot build nukes due to lack of uranium, we are zeroing out nuke flavors and instead prioritizing air/naval superiority and scientific advancement. Ballistics will modernize our ground forces, while increased science and a modest spaceship backup provide alternate paths if domination stalls. We must maintain relentless military pressure against Rome and Arabia to secure their capitals while India is still vulnerable.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Ballistics
        - Rationale: Ballistics unlocks the Machine Gun and Anti-Aircraft Gun, allowing us to upgrade existing Gatling Guns and defend against India's air power. It also opens the path to Rocketry and Combined Arms, keeping our military tech progression alive after Atomic Theory. This is the highest-priority military technology available.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Empiricism
        - Rationale: Empiricism completes the Rationalism branch and provides extraordinary value: +1 Food per citizen (~+216 food empire-wide), +25% Growth, and up to +20% Science in cities from Great Works. This directly addresses our need to grow our population and catch India's science output, while also buffering our culture defense against their tourism.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Current Game State:**
            - Turn 354, Standard speed, Max turns 500 (so about 146 turns left)
            - I'm Portugal (Maria I) with Freedom ideology
            - Era: Modern, currently researching Atomic Theory (11 turns left)
            - Science per turn: 1161
            - Military strength: 5749
            - 10 cities, 216 population
            - Domination progress: 3/8 capitals controlled (Lisbon, Mecca, Carthage)
            - At war with: Rome, Carthage
            - Carthage is nearly defeated - their capital Carthage is occupied and resistance ends soon, Utique is being razed
            - India is the runaway leader: Score 3206, Atomic Era, 61 techs, Military strength 14007, 32 delegates (71% diplomatic victory)
            - The Iroquois are my strong allies (Defensive Pact, DoF, Open Borders) but Order ideology
            - Siam is Freedom ally
            - Rome is at war with me and others, seems to be losing
            
            **Key Victory Conditions Analysis:**
            1. **Domination:** I need 5 more capitals. India alone has 10 cities including their massive capital Delhi. Taking India's capitals seems extremely difficult given their 14007 military strength vs my 5749.
            2. **Science:** India is in Atomic era with 61 techs (I'm at 58 Modern). Hard to catch up.
            3. **Cultural:** India has 6/7 influential civs already. They're very close to cultural victory.
            4. **Diplomatic:** India has 32/45 delegates needed. Almost there.
            5. **Time:** Score victory if nothing else.
            
            **Critical Threats:**
            - India is close to BOTH Cultural and Diplomatic victory (6 influential civs, 32 delegates, UN in 7 turns)
            - They have massive gold (17841, 990 GPT) and military
            - They'll likely win soon unless stopped
            
            **Strategic Options:**
            Since India is the dominant threat and close to multiple victories, I need to consider how to stop them or win first. However, given my much lower military strength and tech position, direct confrontation with India is extremely risky.
            
            Actually, looking more carefully at the diplomatic situation:
            - World Congress voting in 7 turns
            - India has 32 delegates, needs 45
            - There are proposals for Passport System (India), UN (Siam), Byzantine Sphere of Influence: Singapore
            - The UN proposal by Siam could trigger the UN special session if Atomic Theory is reached
            
            Wait, I need to think about this differently. Can I actually win domination? Let's count capitals needed:
            - Delhi (India) - very hard, but if I somehow get it...
            - Constantinople (Byzantium) - allied to me currently but Order
            - Sukhothai (Siam) - Freedom ally, probably shouldn't attack
            - Onondaga (Iroquois) - ally, shouldn't attack
            - Rome (Rome) - at war, doable
            - Damascus (Arabia) - weak, doable
            - Maybe others?
            
            Actually with only ~146 turns left and India being so far ahead, I probably can't win a standard victory unless I do something drastic. But I notice the prompt says "Your goal is to call as many tools as you need to make high-level decisions."
            
            Looking at my current flavor settings, they're extremely war-focused (Offense 100, Mobile 100, Air 100, etc.) with Conquest grand strategy. But India is much stronger.
            
            Let me reconsider: what can I actually do?
            1. Try to push for domination by taking Rome, Arabia, Byzantium capitals - but this will take time and may not be enough
            2. Try to block India's diplomatic victory - but they need 45 votes and have 32, getting 13 more is possible
            3. Try to block India's cultural victory - hard to influence India when they're influencing me
            4. Go for science - probably can't beat India
            5. Just maximize score in case of time victory
            
            Wait, I need to check if there's a way to actually turn this around. Looking at India:
            - 304 population, massive cities
            - Delhi is size 58 with wonders
            - But... do I have nukes? My flavors say Nuke: 100, UseNuke: 90. I have 0 oil and 0 uranium though according to resources. Wait, I have no oil or uranium listed! That means I can't build nukes or many modern units.
            
            Actually wait, I do have Aluminum: 2. But no uranium. So no nukes.
            
            Carthage is basically done. I should finish them off, take the last city. Rome is at war with multiple people including me. The Iroquois are fighting Rome too.
            
            Given the situation, I think I should:
            1. Keep pressing the war against Rome and finish Carthage completely
            2. Not attack India directly yet (too strong)
            3. Try to take more capitals for domination
            4. Build up science and military
            
            However, there's a major diplomatic issue: India is close to diplomatic victory. The UN vote is in 7 turns. But Siam proposed "Enact: United Nations" - if this passes and gets built, it triggers the UN special session. But India needs 45 delegates for World Leader.
            
            Actually looking at city-states, India is allied with many: Panama City, Oslo, Shedet, Riga, Buenos Aires, Eraclea, Bunkeya, Venezia, Bornu, Singapore (actually Singapore is Byzantium ally). Most city-states seem to have India as ally.
            
            Hmm, what if I change my grand strategy? With 146 turns left and India so far ahead in multiple victory types, maybe I should focus on trying to win domination by taking capitals from weaker civs first, then challenging India.
            
            But actually, if India wins culturally or diplomatically soon, the game ends. Cultural victory needs "influence everyone" - they have 6/7. They need to influence me (Portugal) - currently I'm at Exotic (13%) Rising Slowly. Actually wait, the cultural victory in VP requires: influence everyone, get ideology with 2 tier 3 tenets, and finish Citizen Earth Protocol. India has Order with 5 policies, so they might have tier 3 tenets. And they need to influence everyone.
            
            For me to stop cultural victory, I need to generate more culture to resist their tourism. I'm generating 731 culture/turn. That's actually decent.
            
            For diplomatic, I can't really stop them easily.
            
            Let me look at my relationship with India. My opinion: Hostile (-100 public, -100 private). They think my warmongering is an existential threat. We have denounced each other. 
            
            Should I attack India? Their military is 14007 vs my 5749. That's more than double. But I have good allies (Iroquois, Siam, Byzantium). If we all attacked India... The Iroquois have 6093 military. Siam has 3448. Combined that's over 15000, comparable to India. But coordinating that is hard.
            
            Actually, the Iroquois and India are NOT at war. Looking at Iroquois relationships: they have Open Borders and no war with India. Byzantium is friends with India. Siam has Open Borders with India.
            
            So my allies are not going to help me fight India. I'm basically on my own if I attack India.
            
            Given all this, I think I need to:
            1. Finish Carthage completely (burn their remaining cities or keep them)
            2. Keep fighting Rome
            3. Build up science (Atomic Theory -> Electronics/Computers -> Rocketry etc.)
            4. Maybe try to push for Spaceship as a backup?
            5. Focus military production but avoid India for now
            
            Wait, let me reconsider the current war situation with Carthage and Rome.
            
            Carthage: Has only Hippo Regius and Gades left. Their capital Carthage is occupied (resistance ends in 1 turn, but production is 0?). Utique is being razed. War score is 100%, weariness 27%. I should make peace and maybe keep Carthage as a vassal or just finish them. Actually, looking at my relationship with Carthage, they hate me (-100) and are at war. With war score 100%, I could probably force them to capitulate and become a vassal. That would give me a domination victory capital! Wait, I already control Carthage capital. Capitulated vassals count for domination. But Carthage has no cities basically - just Hippo Regius and Gades maybe? Actually they still have Hippo Regius and Gades. Carthage (capital) is occupied by me. If I make peace and make them a vassal, does that count? Yes, vassalized civs count their capital as controlled for domination. But wait, for domination victory in VP, if you vassalize a civ, you control their capital. Since I already captured Carthage, I already control it. Making them a vassal might not add anything new. Actually, I already have Carthage's capital. Vassalizing them might give me their other cities without occupying them.
            
            Rome: At war with me, The Iroquois, Siam, Arabia, Byzantium. Rome is getting crushed. Their war score shows:
            - vs Portugal: 2% (we're winning)
            - vs Iroquois: 0%
            - vs Siam: -6%
            - vs Arabia: 0%
            - vs Byzantium: 0%
            Rome has 11 cities but several are occupied/puppets. I could push for Rome's capital.
            
            Byzantium: Actually they have a DoF with me and are fighting Rome. But they're Order ideology.
            
            Arabia: Only 2 cities, weak. Could be an easy target after Rome.
            
            I think the best path to domination is:
            1. Finish Rome (with Iroquois help)
            2. Take Arabia
            3. Then maybe challenge Byzantium or India
            
            But time is short. Let me reconsider if there's a faster path.
            
            Actually, I just realized: my Nuke and UseNuke flavors are at 100 and 90 respectively, but I have no Uranium! The flavors don't help if I can't build them. I should probably deprioritize nuke-related things.
            
            Also, looking at my current research: Atomic Theory in 11 turns. After that, I could go for:
            - Rocketry (Combustion -> Rocketry? No, Ballistics -> Rocketry)
            - Electronics (Refrigeration -> Electronics)
            - Computers (Refrigeration -> Computers)
            
            For Science victory I'd need a lot more.
            
            What about policies? Next policy in 29 turns. Available policies include Freedom tenets (Level 1: Creative Expression, Civil Society, Economic Union, Avant Garde, Covert Action). Since I'm Freedom and need to counter India's cultural influence, maybe Avant Garde or Creative Expression? Actually for domination/war, none of these are amazing but Civil Society helps growth.
            
            Let's look at what tools I need to call:
            1. set-flavors or keep-status-quo
            2. set-research (next tech after Atomic Theory)
            3. set-policy (next policy when available)
            4. set-relationship (maybe adjust with someone?)
            5. set-persona (current persona is very warmongery, maybe fine but could adjust)
            
            Current flavors are extremely war-focused. Given the time pressure and need to take capitals, keeping the war focus makes sense. But I might want to increase Science a bit since I need better units/tech to fight India. Also reduce Nuke since I can't build them.
            
            For research after Atomic Theory:
            - Atomic Theory unlocks... actually what does it unlock? The options don't say. Looking at the tech options available: Ballistics, Refrigeration, Penicillin. These are Modern era techs. Atomic Theory probably leads to things like Rocketry.
            - Since I want better military and potentially air power, maybe Rocketry path or Electronics?
            - Computers gives... ?
            - Actually, to get better units I might want Ballistics for Machine Guns and Anti-Aircraft, or Rocketry for rockets? Or Radio?
            - Wait, I'm at Modern era. The next era is Atomic. I need Atomic Theory to enter Atomic Era. After that, I can get things like Radar, Nuclear Fission (but no uranium so maybe skip), Rocketry, etc.
            - Actually in Civ 5 VP, the tech tree from Atomic Theory might go to: Radar, Computers, Rocketry, Electronics, etc.
            
            Given I'm already researching Atomic Theory, after it I should probably go for something that helps military or science. Electronics improves trade routes and might give useful stuff. Rocketry gives missiles and maybe space parts eventually.
            
            Actually, looking at my units, I have lots of air power (bombers, triplanes) and naval. My land units are somewhat outdated (Riflemen, Gatling Guns, Cavalry). I need Infantry or Tanks. In Civ 5, Infantry comes with... Plastics? No, Replaceable Parts gives Infantry. Oil gives Tanks (Combustion). Actually looking at my units, I do have Infantry listed in Unit Stats? No, I have Rifleman (50 str) but not Infantry (62 str). Wait, Infantry is listed under Defense with strength 62, so it exists. What tech gives Infantry? Probably Plastics or something.
            
            Actually, in the base game, Infantry is from Replaceable Parts. But this is VP. I don't know the exact VP tech tree. I'll guess Atomic Theory -> maybe leads to advanced military techs.
            
            For policy, since I have Freedom with 3 tenets already (Draft Registration, Capitalism, Universal Suffrage), I should probably get more Freedom tenets. Avant Garde or Economic Union would be good.
            
            For relationships:
            - Keep Iroquois friendly
            - Siam is friendly 
            - Byzantium is "friendly" but my opinion is -50. They're Order and allied with my enemies. I might want to be less friendly or prepare for war.
            - Actually Byzantium has DoF with me but is Order. That -999 penalty means the DoF is fake basically.
            - India is hostile
            - Rome is at war
            - Arabia is weak and Order
            
            I should probably prepare to turn on Byzantium eventually, but keep the peace for now while I finish Rome and Arabia.
            
            Actually wait - for domination victory, do I need to personally capture all capitals, or can my allies/vassals capture them? In Civ 5, you need to control or vassalize all original capitals. So I need to either capture them myself or have them as vassals. If The Iroquois capture Rome, that doesn't help me directly unless Rome becomes their vassal... but then I don't control Rome's capital.
            
            So I need to make sure I capture the capitals myself, or vassalize the civs so their capitals count as controlled by me? Actually in VP, vassalized civs count their original capital as controlled by the master for domination purposes. But if another civ captures a capital and keeps it, it counts for them.
            
            So I should race to capture Rome's capital before the Iroquois do.
            
            For flavors: I need to keep pushing military but maybe adjust. Current flavors have Nuke:100 and UseNuke:90 which is wasteful since I have no uranium. I should reduce those and maybe increase Naval or Air.
            
            Let me also look at my persona. It's extremely aggressive (VictoryCompetitiveness 10, WarBias 10, HostileBias 9, Boldness 10). With India so strong, maybe I should be slightly less reckless? But the goal is to win, and domination requires aggression.
            
            Hmm, actually I wonder if I should consider a tactical nuke option. But with 0 uranium, I literally can't build them. So Nuke flavors should be reduced.
            
            Let's decide on changes:
            
            **set-flavors:**
            - GrandStrategy: Conquest (keep)
            - Reduce Nuke from 100 to 0 or 10
            - Reduce UseNuke from 90 to 0 or 10
            - Increase Science from 50 to 80 (need better tech)
            - Increase Spaceship from 0 to 30 (backup plan)
            - Maybe increase Naval and Air since I'm coastal
            - Keep Offense, Mobilization, MilitaryTraining, Mobile high
            - Maybe increase Growth and Production slightly for economy
            
            Actually, looking at my available options again, I'm not sure what values to set. Let me think about what would help the in-game AI most.
            
            The AI is currently building lots of bombers and dreadnoughts and triplanes, which seems good. It's also pushing Rome.
            
            I should probably:
            - Keep Conquest grand strategy
            - Nuke: 0 (can't build them)
            - UseNuke: 0
            - Science: 80 (faster tech = better units)
            - Spaceship: 40 (in case domination fails)
            - Air: 100 (air power is strong and I have it)
            - Naval: 80 (coastal map, lots of naval action)
            - Offense: 100
            - Mobile: 100
            - Production: 100
            
            **set-research:**
            Next tech after Atomic Theory. Options shown: Ballistics, Refrigeration, Penicillin. But there might be others unlocked by Atomic Theory that aren't listed here because they're not available options from the tool? Actually the tool says I can only use options from the Options section. The Technologies section lists Ballistics, Refrigeration, Penicillin. So my next research must be one of these three!
            
            Wait, that's bizarre. Atomic Theory is a prerequisite for some of these? No, Ballistics requires... what? The description says Ballistics allows Anti-Aircraft Guns and Machine Guns. Refrigeration allows Offshore Platforms and increases trade route distance. Penicillin gives Marines and Medical Labs and Fallout Reduction.
            
            If these are my only options, which is best?
            - Ballistics: Unlocks Machine Gun and Anti-Aircraft Gun. Completing unlocks Rocketry and Combined Arms. This is strong military.
            - Refrigeration: Unlocks Electronics and Computers. Economic/science boost.
            - Penicillin: Marines (amphibious) and Medical Lab (growth). Unlocks Electronics.
            
            Since I'm at war and need better units, Ballistics seems best. Machine Guns would upgrade my Gatling Guns. Anti-Aircraft helps against India's bombers. And it leads to Rocketry.
            
            **set-policy:**
            Options include Level 1 Freedom tenets and others. Since policy is in 29 turns, and I'm Freedom:
            - Avant Garde: +33% Great People, -2 Boredom. Good for overall development.
            - Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Helps against cultural loss? Actually cultural victory is about OUR tourism vs THEIR culture. Since India is winning culturally, boosting my own tourism doesn't stop them from influencing me. It helps me win culture but that's not realistic.
            - Civil Society: Specialists eat 2 less food, farms/plantations/unique improvements +4 food. Growth boost.
            - Economic Union: +2 trade routes, +6 gold from Freedom trade routes. Good for economy.
            - Covert Action: Spy stuff.
            
            Actually, wait. Cultural victory requires me to influence all other civs. But India is already influential over me! I can't win cultural victory. So boosting tourism is only useful to increase my culture defense (wait no, tourism is offense, culture is defense against tourism). To stop India from culturally winning, I need more CULTURE, not tourism. Actually, India's tourism is their offense. My culture per turn is my defense. So Creative Expression (+2 tourism) doesn't help defend against India's tourism. I need more culture or culture defense.
            
            Hmm, but the policy options don't have a pure culture defense option. Actually, policies that increase my culture output help indirectly by increasing my total culture pool.
            
            Wait, I just realized: in Civ 5, your accumulated lifetime culture doesn't matter for defending against tourism. It's your culture PER TURN that generates defensive "culture" against their tourism. So policies that increase culture per turn help defend.
            
            Avant Garde accelerates Great People which could get Great Works or Great Musicians for culture bombs.
            Civil Society increases food which can lead to more population -> more culture.
            
            But none of these are amazing for stopping India's culture.
            
            For my Freedom ideology, I should pick something that helps my strategy. Since I'm warmongering but also need science, maybe Economic Union for more trade routes, or Avant Garde for Great People.
            
            Actually, let me reconsider whether I should even stay in Freedom. Could I switch to Order or Autocracy? No, switching would cause massive unhappiness and I don't have the ability right now. I'm stuck with Freedom.
            
            Among the Freedom options, Economic Union seems solid for gold and trade routes. Avant Garde is also good.
            
            But looking at the list again, "Expertise" is continuing Progress: +15% production toward buildings, +10 culture per building. That's actually amazing for culture defense and production!
            "Shadow Networks" is Statecraft: +25% science from allied city-states, +3% culture in capital per 100 spy points, etc. Could be good but I don't have many city-state allies.
            
            Wait, I don't have these branches completed. My policy branches:
            - Freedom (3/tenets? No, it's a branch with 3 policies adopted but not completed)
            - Industry (1)
            - Rationalism (5) - almost done? Max is usually 6 or 7.
            - Statecraft (2)
            - Imperialism (1)
            - Progress (5)
            - Authority (5)
            
            Actually I can adopt ANY of the listed policies, not just Freedom tenets. The options include continuing branches I'm already in, or new branches. Since I have 5 Rationalism, maybe I should finish Rationalism? But Empiricism is listed and it's Continuing Rationalism. Let's see what's in Rationalism already: Scientific Revolution, Enlightenment, Free Thought, Rights of Man. What's the 6th? Empiricism gives +1 food per citizen and +25% growth, +3% science per Great Work. That's strong for science!
            
            Wait, Rationalism in VP: the opener + 5 policies = 6 total. I already have 5, so Empiricism would be the 6th, completing the branch. Completing Rationalism usually gives a great scientist or science boost.
            
            For domination and overall power, Empiricism seems amazing: +1 food per citizen is huge for a 216 pop empire, and the science boost from Great Works is nice.
            
            Expertise (Progress) - I only have 3 policies in Progress? Wait I listed: Progress, Organization, Liberty, Equality, Fraternity. That's 6! So Progress is completed. Expertise must be from somewhere else? No, Expertise says "Continuing Progress Branch" but if I have 6 Progress policies, I already completed it. Maybe VP Progress has more than 6? Or maybe I miscounted. Let me check: Progress branch in VP has 6 policies: Progress (opener), Organization, Liberty, Equality, Fraternity, and??? Actually I listed 6 including the opener. So it's complete. Then Expertise would be the 7th? Some branches have more.
            
            Hmm, I'll go with Empiricism since completing Rationalism is valuable and the food/science boost is great.
            
            Actually wait, I should double check. My policies listed:
            - Freedom: Draft Registration, Capitalism, Universal Suffrage (3 adopted, but Freedom in VP has 3 tiers, maybe 8-10 total possible)
            - Industry: Industry (just opener, 1)
            - Rationalism: Rationalism, Scientific Revolution, Enlightenment, Free Thought, Rights of Man (5 adopted)
            - Statecraft: Statecraft, Foreign Service (2)
            - Imperialism: Imperialism (1)
            - Progress: Progress, Organization, Liberty, Equality, Fraternity (5? or is Progress the 6th?)
            
            If Progress has 5 and I listed 5 names, maybe I haven't completed it. Actually "Progress" is the branch name and usually the first policy is also called "Progress." So I have the opener + 4 more = 5 total. That might not be complete if the branch has 6. Or 7.
            
            Actually, I'll stick with Empiricism for next policy. It provides huge growth and science which I need to compete with India.
            
            **set-relationship:**
            Should I change any relationships?
            - Byzantium: Currently Public -50, Private -50. They have DoF with me but are Order and friends with India. They also have Open Borders. I should probably keep the war preparation but not declare yet. Maybe increase hostility slightly so the AI considers war? Or decrease it to avoid being backstabbed? Actually with -50 already, the AI might backstab me. Let me leave it.
            - Arabia: Public 0, Private 0. But I captured Mecca (their former capital). They denounced me. Real approach is Hostile. Maybe I should set this to war-like to prepare for taking Damascus? Actually I'm already at war with others, maybe I shouldn't open another front. Let me leave Arabia for now.
            - Everyone else seems appropriate.
            
            Actually, I should check if I need to denounce anyone to prepare for war. But my persona already has high DenounceWillingness (10).
            
            **set-persona:**
            Current persona is extremely aggressive. Given that I'm in multiple wars and need to take 5 more capitals, keeping aggression high makes sense. But maybe reduce WonderCompetitiveness further or adjust MinorCivCompetitiveness.
            
            Wait, I should reconsider whether domination is viable. Let's do the math:
            - India: ~14007 military, 304 pop, 10 cities, Atomic era. Beatable? Maybe with concentrated force, but they're far away.
            - Rome: ~4817 military, but being crushed by multiple civs. Their capital is nearby. Beatable.
            - Arabia: ~819 military, 2 cities. Easy.
            - Byzantium: ~3222 military, 9 cities. Moderate.
            - Siam: ~3448 military, 13 cities. Could be tough but they're Freedom like me.
            - The Iroquois: ~6093 military, 12 cities. Ally.
            - Me: 5749 military, 10 cities.
            
            If I capture Rome (capital), Arabia (Damascus), Byzantium (Constantinople), and somehow India's Delhi... that's 4. Plus I already have Lisbon, Mecca, Carthage. That would be 7. I'd still need Siam or Iroquois or someone else. There are 8 capitals needed total (since 8 major civs). Wait, victory progress says "CapitalsNeeded: 8". There are 8 major civs including me. So I need all 8 original capitals. I have 3. Need 5 more. The remaining are: Rome, Istanbul (Byzantium), Delhi (India), Sukhothai (Siam), Onondaga (Iroquois), Damascus (Arabia).
            
            I cannot take Onondaga (Iroquois are my allies and I don't want to break that). I probably shouldn't take Sukhothai (Siam is my Freedom ally). That leaves: Rome, Byzantium, India, Arabia. That's 4. I'd still need 1 more. I could take Siam or Iroquois, but that would be painful diplomatically.
            
            Wait, are Siam and Iroquois capitals required? Yes, for domination I need ALL original capitals. So eventually I'd have to betray my allies. But maybe that's the only path.
            
            With 146 turns left, capturing 5 capitals including Delhi is very hard. Delhi is massively defended and far away.
            
            Alternative: Can I win via Time victory? My score is 2356 vs India's 3206. India is winning.
            
            So really, do I have any path to victory? Maybe not. But I should still play to maximize my position.
            
            Actually, the best chance might be to pivot toward a Science victory or Diplomatic. But India is ahead in both.
            
            Science: Unlocked in Information era. I need to reach Information era and build spaceship parts. India is already in Atomic and has way more science. Unlikely.
            
            Diplomatic: India has 32/45 delegates. Almost impossible to catch.
            
            Culture: India has 6/7 influential. They just need to influence me more and finish Citizen Earth Protocol. Wait, Citizen Earth Protocol is a world wonder or a national wonder? In VP, cultural victory requires building the "Citizen Earth Protocol" wonder which becomes available late. If I can stop India from building it... but it's a world wonder and India has massive production.
            
            Actually looking at India's cities, Delhi has tons of wonders but I don't see Citizen Earth Protocol in the list. So maybe they haven't built it yet. Cultural victory also requires an ideology with 2 Tier 3 tenets. India has Order with 5 policies. In Order, Tier 3 tenets are at the bottom. With 5 policies, they might not have 2 Tier 3 yet (Order might need 7-8 policies total for 2 tier 3s, depending on prerequisites). This means India might be close culturally but not done yet.
            
            What if I try to rush the Citizen Earth Protocol myself? No, I'm not influential over anyone.
            
            Hmm. Given all this, I think my best bet is:
            1. Keep fighting and hope something breaks my way
            2. Focus on science to try to stay competitive
            3. Build military to potentially challenge India if they overextend
            4. Maybe try to capture Delhi if an opportunity arises

So if I set Carthage to +50, the AI is more likely to accept peace. But Carthage has 100% war weariness too, so they'd probably accept peace anyway if I offered. Let me try to improve the relationship with Carthage slightly to make peace more acceptable. But Carthage's opinion of me is -100 (public). Their leader expressed -100. So they hate me.
